   129→def _default_blind_packet_path() -> Path:
   130→    return get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "review_packet_blind.json"
   131→
   132→
   133→def _is_sha256_hex(raw: object) -> bool:
   134→    return (
   135→        isinstance(raw, str)
   136→        and len(raw) == 64
   137→        and all(ch in "0123456789abcdefABCDEF" for ch in raw)
   138→    )
   139→
   140→
   141→def _hash_file_sha256(path: Path) -> str | None:
   142→    try:
   143→        data = path.read_bytes()
   144→    except OSError:
   145→        return None
   146→    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()
   147→
   148→
   149→def _resolve_packet_path(raw_path: object) -> Path | None:
   150→    if not isinstance(raw_path, str):
   151→        return None
   152→    text = raw_path.strip()
   153→    if not text:
   154→        return None
   155→    path = Path(text)
   156→    return path if path.is_absolute() else get_project_root() / path
   157→
   158→
   159→def _assessment_provenance_status(
   160→    findings_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   161→    *,
   162→    import_file: str,
   163→) -> AssessmentProvenanceModel:
   164→    """Evaluate whether assessments come from a trusted blind batch artifact."""
   165→    provenance = findings_data["provenance"]
   166→    if not provenance:
   167→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   168→            trusted=False,
   169→            reason="missing provenance metadata",
   170→            import_file=import_file,
   171→        )
   172→
   173→    kind = str(provenance.get("kind", "")).strip()
   174→    if kind != _BLIND_PROVENANCE_KIND:
   175→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   176→            trusted=False,
   177→            reason=f"unsupported provenance kind: {kind or '<missing>'}",
   178→            import_file=import_file,
   179→        )
   180→
   181→    if provenance.get("blind") is not True:
   182→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   183→            trusted=False,
   184→            reason="provenance is not marked blind=true",
   185→            import_file=import_file,
   186→        )
   187→
   188→    runner = str(provenance.get("runner", "")).strip().lower()
   189→    if runner not in _SUPPORTED_BLIND_REVIEW_RUNNERS:
   190→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   191→            trusted=False,
   192→            reason=f"unsupported runner in provenance: {runner or '<missing>'}",
   193→            import_file=import_file,
   194→        )
   195→
   196→    packet_hash = provenance.get("packet_sha256")
   197→    if not _is_sha256_hex(packet_hash):
   198→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   199→            trusted=False,
   200→            reason="missing or invalid packet_sha256 in provenance",
   201→            import_file=import_file,
   202→        )
   203→
   204→    packet_path = _resolve_packet_path(provenance.get("packet_path"))
   205→    if packet_path is None:
   206→        packet_path = _default_blind_packet_path()
   207→    if not packet_path.exists():
   208→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   209→            trusted=False,
   210→            reason=f"blind packet not found: {packet_path}",
   211→            import_file=import_file,
   212→        )
   213→    observed_hash = _hash_file_sha256(packet_path)
   214→    if observed_hash is None:
   215→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   216→            trusted=False,
   217→            reason=f"unable to hash blind packet: {packet_path}",
   218→            import_file=import_file,
   219→        )
   220→    if observed_hash != packet_hash:
   221→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   222→            trusted=False,
   223→            reason=(
   224→                "blind packet hash mismatch "
   225→                f"(expected {packet_hash[:12]}..., got {observed_hash[:12]}...)"
   226→            ),
   227→            import_file=import_file,
   228→        )
   229→
   230→    return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   231→        trusted=True,
   232→        reason="trusted blind subagent provenance",
   233→        runner=runner,
   234→        packet_path=str(packet_path),
   235→        packet_sha256=packet_hash,
   236→        import_file=import_file,
   237→    )
   238→
   239→
   240→def resolve_override_context(
   241→    *,
   242→    manual_override: bool,
   243→    manual_attest: str | None,
   244→    assessment_override: bool,
   245→    assessment_note: str | None,
   246→) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
   247→    """Support legacy assessment_* flags while preferring manual_* naming."""
   248→    override = bool(manual_override or assessment_override)
   249→    attest = (
   250→        manual_attest
   251→        if isinstance(manual_attest, str) and manual_attest.strip()
   252→        else assessment_note
   253→    )
   254→    if isinstance(attest, str):
   255→        attest = attest.strip()
   256→    return override, attest
   257→
   258→
   259→def _validate_attested_external_attestation(attest: str | None) -> str | None:
   260→    """Validate and normalize attestation text for attested external imports."""
   261→    if not isinstance(attest, str) or not attest.strip():
   262→        return None
   263→    text = attest.strip()
   264→    lowered = text.lower()
   265→    if all(phrase in lowered for phrase in _ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_REQUIRED_PHRASES):
   266→        return text
   267→    return None
   268→
   269→
   270→def _print_import_error_hints(
   271→    errors: list[str],
   272→    *,
   273→    import_file: str,
   274→    colorize_fn,
   275→) -> None:
   276→    """Print actionable retry commands for common import policy failures."""
   277→    joined = " ".join(err.lower() for err in errors)
   278→    quoted_import = shlex.quote(import_file)
   279→    import_cmd = (
   280→        "desloppify review --import "
   281→        f"{quoted_import} --attested-external --attest "
   282→        f"\"{_ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_ATTEST_EXAMPLE}\""
   283→    )
   284→    validate_cmd = (
   285→        "desloppify review --validate-import "
   286→        f"{quoted_import} --attested-external --attest "
   287→        f"\"{_ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_ATTEST_EXAMPLE}\""
   288→    )
   289→    findings_only_cmd = f"desloppify review --import {quoted_import}"
   290→
   291→    if "--attested-external requires --attest containing both" in joined:
   292→        print(
   293→            colorize_fn(
   294→                "  Hint: rerun with the required attestation template:",
   295→                "yellow",
   296→            ),
   297→            file=sys.stderr,
   298→        )
   299→        print(colorize_fn(f"    `{import_cmd}`", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   300→        print(
   301→            colorize_fn(
   302→                f"  Preflight without state changes: `{validate_cmd}`",
   303→                "dim",
   304→            ),
   305→            file=sys.stderr,
   306→        )
   307→        return
   308→
   309→    if (
   310→        "--attested-external requires valid blind packet provenance" in joined
   311→        or "supports runner='claude'" in joined
   312→    ):
   313→        print(
   314→            colorize_fn(
   315→                "  Hint: if provenance is valid, rerun with:",
   316→                "yellow",
   317→            ),
   318→            file=sys.stderr,
   319→        )
   320→        print(colorize_fn(f"    `{import_cmd}`", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   321→        print(
   322→            colorize_fn(
   323→                f"  Preflight without state changes: `{validate_cmd}`",
   324→                "dim",
   325→            ),
   326→            file=sys.stderr,
   327→        )
   328→        print(
   329→            colorize_fn(
   330→                f"  Findings-only fallback: `{findings_only_cmd}`",
   331→                "dim",
   332→            ),
   333→            file=sys.stderr,
   334→        )
   335→
   336→
   337→def _apply_assessment_import_policy(
   338→    findings_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   339→    *,
   340→    import_file: str,
   341→    attested_external: bool,
   342→    attested_attest: str | None,
   343→    manual_override: bool,
   344→    manual_attest: str | None,
   345→    trusted_assessment_source: bool,
   346→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None,
   347→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   348→    """Apply trust gating for assessment imports (findings import always allowed)."""
   349→    assessments = findings_data["assessments"]
   350→    has_assessments = bool(assessments)
   351→    assessment_count = len(assessments) if has_assessments else 0
   352→    provenance_status = _assessment_provenance_status(
   353→        findings_data, import_file=import_file
   354→    )
   355→    policy = AssessmentImportPolicyModel(
   356→        assessments_present=has_assessments,
   357→        assessment_count=int(assessment_count),
   358→        trusted=False,
   359→        mode="none",
   360→        reason="",
   361→        provenance=provenance_status,
   362→    )
   363→
   364→    def _attach_policy(payload: ReviewImportPayload) -> ReviewImportPayload:
   365→        normalized = dict(payload)
   366→        normalized[_ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = policy.to_dict()
   367→        return normalized
   368→
   369→    if not has_assessments:
   370→        return _attach_policy(findings_data), []
   371→
   372→    if trusted_assessment_source:
   373→        policy = replace(
   374→            policy,
   375→            mode="trusted_internal",
   376→            trusted=True,
   377→            reason=(trusted_assessment_label or "trusted internal run-batches import"),
   378→        )
   379→        return _attach_policy(findings_data), []
   380→
   381→    if attested_external:
   382→        normalized_attest = _validate_attested_external_attestation(attested_attest)
   383→        if normalized_attest is None:
   384→            return None, [
   385→                "--attested-external requires --attest containing both "
   386→                "'without awareness' and 'unbiased'"
   387→            ]
   388→        if provenance_status.trusted is not True:
   389→            return None, [
   390→                "--attested-external requires valid blind packet provenance "
   391→                f"(current status: {provenance_status.reason or 'untrusted provenance'})"
   392→            ]
   393→        runner = provenance_status.runner.strip().lower()
   394→        if runner not in _ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_RUNNERS:
   395→            return None, [
   396→                "--attested-external currently supports runner='claude' provenance only"
   397→            ]
   398→        policy = replace(
   399→            policy,
   400→            mode="attested_external",
   401→            trusted=True,
   402→            reason="attested external blind subagent provenance",
   403→            attest=normalized_attest,
   404→        )
   405→        return _attach_policy(findings_data), []
   406→
   407→    if manual_override:
   408→        if not isinstance(manual_attest, str) or not manual_attest.strip():
   409→            return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   410→        policy = replace(
   411→            policy,
   412→            mode="manual_override",
   413→            reason="manual override attested by operator",
   414→            attest=manual_attest.strip(),
   415→        )
   416→        return _attach_policy(findings_data), []
   417→
   418→    policy = replace(policy, mode="findings_only")
   419→    if findings_data["provenance"]:
   420→        provenance_reason = provenance_status.reason.strip()
   421→        if provenance_status.trusted is True:
   422→            policy = replace(
   423→                policy,
   424→                reason=(
   425→                    "external imports cannot self-attest trust even when provenance appears valid; "
   426→                    "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   427→                ),
   428→            )
   429→        elif provenance_reason:
   430→            policy = replace(
   431→                policy,
   432→                reason=(
   433→                    "external imports cannot self-attest trust "
   434→                    f"({provenance_reason}); run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   435→                ),
   436→            )
   437→        else:
   438→            policy = replace(
   439→                policy,
   440→                reason=(
   441→                    "external imports cannot self-attest trust; "
   442→                    "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   443→                ),
   444→            )
   445→    else:
   446→        policy = replace(
   447→            policy,
   448→            reason="missing trusted run-batches source; imported findings only",
   449→        )
   450→    payload = dict(findings_data)
   451→    payload["assessments"] = {}
   452→    payload[_ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = policy.to_dict()
   453→    return payload, []
   454→
   455→
   456→def _has_non_empty_strings(items: object) -> bool:
   457→    """Return True when ``items`` is a list with at least one non-empty string."""
   458→    return isinstance(items, list) and any(
   459→        isinstance(item, str) and item.strip() for item in items
   460→    )
   461→
   462→
   463→def _validate_holistic_findings_schema(
   464→    findings_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   465→    *,
   466→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   467→) -> list[str]:
   468→    """Validate strict holistic finding schema expected by issue import."""
   469→    findings = findings_data["findings"]
   470→
   471→    allowed_dimensions: set[str] = set()
   472→    if isinstance(lang_name, str) and lang_name.strip():
   473→        _, dimension_prompts, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
   474→        allowed_dimensions = set(dimension_prompts)
   475→
   476→    errors: list[str] = []
   477→    for idx, entry in enumerate(findings):
   478→        _normalized: ReviewFindingPayload | None
   479→        _normalized, entry_errors = validate_review_finding_payload(
   480→            entry,
   481→            label=f"findings[{idx}]",
   482→            allowed_dimensions=allowed_dimensions or None,
   483→            allow_dismissed=True,
   484→        )
   485→        for message in entry_errors:
   486→            if (
   487→                "is not allowed" in message
   488→                and lang_name
   489→                and "dimension '" in message
   490→            ):
   491→                message = message.replace(
   492→                    "is not allowed",
   493→                    f"is not valid for language '{lang_name}'",
   494→                )
   495→            errors.append(message)
   496→    return errors
   497→
   498→
   499→def _feedback_dimensions_from_findings(findings: object) -> set[str]:
   500→    """Return dimensions with explicit improvement guidance in findings payload."""
   501→    if not isinstance(findings, list):
   502→        return set()
   503→    dims: set[str] = set()
   504→    for entry in findings:
   505→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   506→            continue
   507→        dim = entry.get("dimension")
   508→        if not isinstance(dim, str) or not dim.strip():
   509→            continue
   510→        suggestion = entry.get("suggestion")
   511→        if isinstance(suggestion, str) and suggestion.strip():
   512→            dims.add(dim.strip())
   513→    return dims
   514→
   515→
   516→def _feedback_dimensions_from_dimension_notes(dimension_notes: object) -> set[str]:
   517→    """Return dimensions with concrete review evidence in dimension_notes payload."""
   518→    if not isinstance(dimension_notes, dict):
   519→        return set()
   520→    dims: set[str] = set()
   521→    for dim, note in dimension_notes.items():
   522→        if not isinstance(dim, str) or not dim.strip():
   523→            continue
   524→        if not isinstance(note, dict):
   525→            continue
   526→        if not _has_non_empty_strings(note.get("evidence")):
   527→            continue
   528→        dims.add(dim.strip())
   529→    return dims
   530→
   531→
   532→def _validate_assessment_feedback(
   533→    findings_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   534→) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
   535→    """Return dimensions missing required feedback and required low-score findings."""
   536→    assessments = findings_data["assessments"]
   537→    if not assessments:
   538→        return [], []
   539→
   540→    finding_dims = _feedback_dimensions_from_findings(findings_data["findings"])
   541→    feedback_dims = set(finding_dims)
   542→    feedback_dims.update(
   543→        _feedback_dimensions_from_dimension_notes(findings_data["dimension_notes"])
   544→    )
   545→    missing_feedback: list[str] = []
   546→    missing_low_score_findings: list[str] = []
   547→    for dim_name, payload in assessments.items():
   548→        if not isinstance(dim_name, str) or not dim_name.strip():
   549→            continue
   550→        score = coerce_assessment_score(payload)
   551→        if score is None:
   552→            continue
   553→        if score_requires_dimension_finding(score) and dim_name not in finding_dims:
   554→            missing_low_score_findings.append(f"{dim_name} ({score:.1f})")
   555→        if score_requires_explicit_feedback(score) and dim_name not in feedback_dims:
   556→            missing_feedback.append(f"{dim_name} ({score:.1f})")
   557→    return sorted(missing_feedback), sorted(missing_low_score_findings)
   558→
   559→
   560→def _parse_and_validate_import(
   561→    import_file: str,
   562→    *,
   563→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   564→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   565→    trusted_assessment_source: bool = False,
   566→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None = None,
   567→    attested_external: bool = False,
   568→    manual_override: bool = False,
   569→    manual_attest: str | None = None,
   570→    assessment_override: bool = False,
   571→    assessment_note: str | None = None,
   572→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   573→    """Parse and validate a review import file (pure function).
   574→
   575→    Returns ``(data, errors)`` where *data* is the normalized payload on
   576→    success, or ``None`` when errors prevent import.
   577→    """
   578→    findings_path = Path(import_file)
   579→    if not findings_path.exists():
   580→        return None, [f"file not found: {import_file}"]
   581→    try:
   582→        findings_data = json.loads(findings_path.read_text())
   583→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   584→        return None, [f"error reading findings: {exc}"]
   585→
   586→    if isinstance(findings_data, list):
   587→        findings_data = {"findings": findings_data}
   588→
   589→    if not isinstance(findings_data, dict):
   590→        return None, ["findings file must contain a JSON array or object"]
   591→
   592→    if "findings" not in findings_data:
   593→        return None, ["findings object must contain a 'findings' key"]
   594→    normalized_findings_data, shape_errors = _normalize_import_payload_shape(
   595→        findings_data
   596→    )
   597→    if shape_errors:
   598→        return None, shape_errors
   599→    assert normalized_findings_data is not None
   600→
   601→    override_enabled, override_attest = resolve_override_context(
   602→        manual_override=manual_override,
   603→        manual_attest=manual_attest,
   604→        assessment_override=assessment_override,
   605→        assessment_note=assessment_note,
   606→    )
   607→    if attested_external and override_enabled:
   608→        return None, [
   609→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --manual-override"
   610→        ]
   611→    if attested_external and allow_partial:
   612→        return None, [
   613→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   614→            "attested score imports require fully valid findings payloads"
   615→        ]
   616→    if override_enabled and allow_partial:
   617→        return None, [
   618→            "--manual-override cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   619→            "manual score imports require fully valid findings payloads"
   620→        ]
   621→    findings_data, policy_errors = _apply_assessment_import_policy(
   622→        normalized_findings_data,
   623→        import_file=import_file,
   624→        attested_external=attested_external,
   625→        attested_attest=override_attest,
   626→        manual_override=override_enabled,
   627→        manual_attest=override_attest,
   628→        trusted_assessment_source=trusted_assessment_source,
   629→        trusted_assessment_label=trusted_assessment_label,
   630→    )
   631→    if policy_errors:
   632→        return None, policy_errors
   633→    assert findings_data is not None
   634→
   635→    missing_feedback, missing_low_score_findings = _validate_assessment_feedback(
   636→        findings_data
   637→    )
   638→    if missing_low_score_findings:
   639→        if override_enabled:
   640→            if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   641→                return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   642→            return findings_data, []
   643→        return None, [
   644→            f"assessments below {LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include at "
   645→            "least one finding for that same dimension with a concrete suggestion. "
   646→            f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_low_score_findings)}"
   647→        ]
   648→
   649→    if missing_feedback:
   650→        if override_enabled:
   651→            if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   652→                return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   653→            return findings_data, []
   654→        return None, [
   655→            f"assessments below {ASSESSMENT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include explicit feedback "
   656→            "(finding with same dimension and non-empty suggestion, or "
   657→            "dimension_notes evidence for that dimension). "
   658→            f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_feedback)}"
   659→        ]
   660→
   661→    schema_errors = _validate_holistic_findings_schema(
   662→        findings_data,
   663→        lang_name=lang_name,
   664→    )
   665→    if schema_errors and not allow_partial:
   666→        visible_errors = schema_errors[:10]
   667→        remaining = len(schema_errors) - len(visible_errors)
   668→        errors = [
   669→            "findings schema validation failed for holistic import. "
   670→            "Fix payload or rerun with --allow-partial to continue."
   671→        ]
   672→        errors.extend(visible_errors)
   673→        if remaining > 0:
   674→            errors.append(f"... {remaining} additional schema error(s) omitted")
   675→        return None, errors
   676→
   677→    return findings_data, []
   678→
   679→
   680→def load_import_findings_data(
   681→    import_file: str,
   682→    *,
   683→    colorize_fn=None,
   684→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   685→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   686→    trusted_assessment_source: bool = False,
   687→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None = None,
   688→    attested_external: bool = False,
   689→    manual_override: bool = False,
   690→    manual_attest: str | None = None,
   691→    assessment_override: bool = False,
   692→    assessment_note: str | None = None,
   693→) -> ReviewImportPayload:
   694→    """Load and normalize review import payload to object format.
   695→
   696→    Raises ``ImportPayloadLoadError`` when validation fails.
   697→    """
   698→    data, errors = _parse_and_validate_import(
   699→        import_file,
   700→        lang_name=lang_name,
   701→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   702→        trusted_assessment_source=trusted_assessment_source,
   703→        trusted_assessment_label=trusted_assessment_label,
   704→        attested_external=attested_external,
   705→        manual_override=manual_override,
   706→        manual_attest=manual_attest,
   707→        assessment_override=assessment_override,
   708→        assessment_note=assessment_note,
   709→    )
   710→    if errors:
   711→        raise ImportPayloadLoadError(errors)
   712→    assert data is not None  # guaranteed when errors is empty
   713→    return data
   714→
   715→
   716→def print_import_load_errors(
   717→    errors: list[str],
   718→    *,
   719→    import_file: str,
   720→    colorize_fn,
   721→) -> None:
   722→    """Print import payload validation errors and actionable hints."""
   723→    for err in errors:
   724→        print(colorize_fn(f"  Error: {err}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   725→    _print_import_error_hints(errors, import_file=import_file, colorize_fn=colorize_fn)
   726→
   727→
   728→def assessment_policy_from_payload(payload: ReviewImportPayload) -> AssessmentImportPolicy:
   729→    """Return parsed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   730→    policy = payload[_ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY]
   731→    if isinstance(policy, dict):
   732→        return policy
   733→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel().to_dict()
   734→
   735→
   736→def assessment_policy_model_from_payload(
   737→    payload: ReviewImportPayload,
   738→) -> AssessmentImportPolicyModel:
   739→    """Return typed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   740→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(assessment_policy_from_payload(payload))
   741→
   742→
   743→def assessment_mode_label(policy: AssessmentImportPolicy) -> str:
   744→    """Return a user-facing label for the selected assessment import mode."""
   745→    mode = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy).mode.strip().lower()
   746→    return _ASSESSMENT_MODE_LABELS.get(mode, f"unknown ({mode or 'none'})")
   747→
   748→
   749→def print_assessment_mode_banner(
   750→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicy,
   751→    *,
   752→    colorize_fn,
   753→) -> None:
   754→    """Print the selected assessment import mode to make policy explicit."""
   755→    policy_model = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy)
   756→    mode = policy_model.mode.strip().lower()
   757→    assessments_present = bool(policy_model.assessments_present)
   758→    if not assessments_present and mode == "none":
   759→        return
   760→    style = "yellow" if mode in {"manual_override", "findings_only"} else "dim"
   761→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Assessment import mode: {assessment_mode_label(policy)}", style))
   762→
   763→
   764→def print_assessment_policy_notice(
   765→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicy,
   766→    *,
   767→    import_file: str,
   768→    colorize_fn,
   769→) -> None:
   770→    """Render trust/override status for assessment-bearing imports."""
   771→    policy_model = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy)
   772→    if not policy_model.assessments_present:
   773→        return
   774→    mode = policy_model.mode.strip().lower()
   775→    reason = policy_model.reason.strip()
   776→
   777→    if mode == "trusted":
   778→        packet_path = policy_model.provenance.packet_path.strip() or None

   862→
   863→
   864→def print_skipped_validation_details(diff: dict[str, Any], *, colorize_fn) -> None:
   865→    """Print validation warnings for skipped imported findings."""
   866→    n_skipped = diff.get("skipped", 0)
   867→    if n_skipped <= 0:
   868→        return
   869→    print(
   870→        colorize_fn(
   871→            f"\n  \u26a0 {n_skipped} finding(s) skipped (validation errors):",
   872→            "yellow",
   873→        )
   874→    )
   875→    for detail in diff.get("skipped_details", []):
   876→        reasons = detail["missing"]
   877→        missing_fields = [r for r in reasons if not r.startswith("invalid ")]
   878→        validation_errors = [r for r in reasons if r.startswith("invalid ")]
   879→        parts = []
   880→        if missing_fields:
   881→            parts.append(f"missing {', '.join(missing_fields)}")
   882→        parts.extend(validation_errors)
   883→        print(
   884→            colorize_fn(
   885→                f"    #{detail['index']} ({detail['identifier']}): {'; '.join(parts)}",
   886→                "yellow",
   887→            )
   888→        )
   889→
   890→
   891→def print_assessments_summary(state: dict[str, Any], *, colorize_fn) -> None:
   892→    """Print holistic subjective assessment summary when present."""
   893→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}
   894→    if not assessments:
   895→        return
   896→    parts = [
   897→        f"{key.replace('_', ' ')} {value['score']}"
   898→        for key, value in sorted(assessments.items())
   899→    ]
   900→    print(colorize_fn(f"\n  Assessments: {', '.join(parts)}", "bold"))
   901→
   902→
   903→def print_open_review_summary(state: dict[str, Any], *, colorize_fn) -> str:
   904→    """Print current open review finding count and return next command."""
   905→    open_review = [
   906→        finding
   907→        for finding in state["findings"].values()
   908→        if finding["status"] == "open" and finding.get("detector") == "review"
   909→    ]
   910→    if not open_review:
   911→        return "desloppify scan"
   912→    print(
   913→        colorize_fn(
   914→            f"\n  {len(open_review)} review issue{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open total "
   915→            f"({len(open_review)} review finding{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open total)",
   916→            "bold",
   917→        )
   918→    )
   919→    print(colorize_fn("  Run `desloppify show review --status open` to see the work queue", "dim"))
   920→    return "desloppify show review --status open"
   921→
   922→
   923→def print_review_import_scores_and_integrity(
   924→    state: dict[str, Any],
   925→    config: dict[str, Any],
   926→    *,
   927→    state_mod,
   928→    target_strict_score_from_config_fn,
   929→    subjective_at_target_fn,
   930→    subjective_rerun_command_fn,
   931→    colorize_fn,
   932→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   933→    """Print subjective integrity warnings (score line handled by print_score_update)."""
   934→    target_strict = target_strict_score_from_config_fn(config, fallback=95.0)
   935→    at_target = subjective_at_target_fn(
   936→        state,
   937→        state.get("dimension_scores", {}),
   938→        target=target_strict,
   939→    )
   940→    if not at_target:
   941→        return []
   942→
   943→    command = subjective_rerun_command_fn(at_target, max_items=5)
   944→    count = len(at_target)
   945→    if count >= 2:
   946→        print(
   947→            colorize_fn(
   948→                "  WARNING: "
   949→                f"{count} subjective scores match the target score. "
   950→                "On the next scan, those dimensions will be reset to 0.0 by the anti-gaming safeguard "
   951→                f"unless you rerun and re-import objective reviews first: {command}",
   952→                "red",
   953→            )
   954→        )
   955→    else:
   956→        print(
   957→            colorize_fn(
   958→                "  WARNING: "
   959→                f"{count} subjective score matches the target score, indicating a high risk of gaming. "
   960→                f"Can you rerun it by running {command} taking extra care to be objective.",
   961→                "yellow",
   962→            )
   963→        )
   964→    return at_target
   965→
   966→
   967→__all__ = [
   968→    "ImportPayloadLoadError",
   969→    "assessment_mode_label",
   970→    "assessment_policy_model_from_payload",
   971→    "assessment_policy_from_payload",
   972→    "load_import_findings_data",
   973→    "print_assessment_mode_banner",
   974→    "print_import_load_errors",

   978→    "print_review_import_scores_and_integrity",
   979→    "print_skipped_validation_details",
   980→    "resolve_override_context",
   981→]
   982→

The fix is **fully done** — and well-tested.

Here is what the investigation found:

---

**Issue 167 verdict: FULLY DONE**

The entire fix described in the issue has been implemented. Here is a breakdown:

**What the issue reported:**
- `show security` and `show duplication` incorrectly printed `'security' is a subjective dimension — its score comes from design reviews, not code findings.`
- Root cause: the branch dispatched to the subjective handler whenever a `display_name` existed in `dimension_scores`, regardless of whether the dimension was actually mechanical.

**What was implemented:**

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/scope.py`** — A new `ResolvedEntity` dataclass and `resolve_entity()` function (lines 17–90) that classifies patterns with explicit priority:
   - Step 1: Special views (`concerns`, `subjective`, `subjective_review`)
   - Step 2: Mechanical dimensions via `scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS` — returns `is_subjective=False`
   - Step 3: Subjective dimensions — only marked `is_subjective=True` if `subjective_assessment` appears in their `detectors` map
   - Step 4: Everything else as passthrough

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/cmd.py`** — The dispatch logic in `cmd_show()` (lines 237–251) now uses `entity.is_subjective` to route correctly:
   - `entity.kind == "dimension" and entity.is_subjective` → `_render_subjective_dimension()`
   - `entity.kind == "dimension" and not entity.is_subjective` → loads findings from all dimension detectors; if empty → `_render_clean_mechanical_dimension()` which prints `"No open findings for Security. Detectors: security, cycles"` without any "subjective" mention

3. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cmd_show.py`** — Tests covering exactly the scenarios from the issue:
   - `test_show_mechanical_dimension_not_labeled_subjective` (line 478): verifies `"Security: 100.0% health"` appears, `"No open findings for Security"` appears, and `"subjective dimension"` does NOT appear
   - `test_show_mechanical_dimension_with_findings` (line 522): verifies findings from both `security` and `cycles` detectors are surfaced together under `show security`
   - `TestResolveEntity::test_security_is_mechanical_dimension` (line 592): verifies `security` resolves to `kind="dimension"`, `is_subjective=False`, with `security` and `cycles` in `detectors`
   - `TestResolveEntity::test_duplication_is_mechanical_dimension` (line 634): verifies `duplication` resolves similarly with `dupes` in `detectors`

author:	peteromallet
association:	owner
edited:	false
status:	none
--
Context from running this against Headless-Wan2GP today (2026-02-26):

- Current state after fresh scan/plan: overall/strict 69.9, objective 91.2, 2559 open.
- Open review findings are now 25 (T1:21, T2:4), and Tier 1 overall is 24 (includes 3 non-review unused-import items).

Why this issue matters in practice (not just UX preference):

I compared an existing manual 10-step cleanup plan (architecture-first, gated) against current `desloppify plan` output. The manual plan tracked 10 architecture IDs as core blockers. In current state, only 2 of those IDs are still open (`8cbb31c0`, `64171d92`); the other 8 are present only as historical/all-state items marked “not reported in latest holistic re-import.”

That is exactly the failure mode of snapshot-only planning: the queue is accurate *now*, but strategy context evaporates when findings are re-imported/rephrased/superseded. Engineers lose continuity and end up re-deriving intent each cycle.

The practical advantage of the manual framework wasn’t “more process”; it was durable context under finding churn:

1) It grouped work by root-cause epics (seams/boundaries/ownership), so progress remained legible even when individual finding IDs changed.
2) It carried explicit rationale and completion gates, so “what changed” didn’t erase “why this matters.”
3) It prevented priority whiplash from raw volume shifts (e.g., very large detector queues that are not necessarily top architectural blockers).

So +1 on Active Planning Mode. The key value is preserving planning memory across scans/imports.

If helpful, the highest-leverage behavior to preserve is: when a source finding disappears, keep the plan item alive as `superseded/needs-remap` with previous rationale and candidate replacement links, instead of silently dropping planning context.

--
author:	peteromallet
association:	owner
edited:	false
status:	none
--
+1 more thing to make this really work in practice: we should cluster items, not just reorder them.

Specifically, cluster both when we build the plan and when we push/update plan items from scan/next/review.

Why: flat item lists still create churn/whiplash even with a living doc. Clusters preserve strategy context and make progress legible across finding ID churn.

Suggested clustering axes:
- root cause / epic (boundary leak, ownership confusion, duplicated orchestration, etc.)
- architectural seam or package area
- dependency chain (what must land first)

If an underlying finding is superseded, keep the cluster and remap members instead of dropping context. That keeps planning memory intact while details evolve.
--
```

**Verdict: Not started.**

Issue 140 requests a `--by-language` mode for multi-language repositories that would produce:
1. Per-language score blocks in CLI output (scan + status)
2. Per-language scorecard image files (e.g. `scorecard-go.png`, `scorecard-swift.png`)
3. A `--by-language` flag on both `scan` and `status`
4. Deterministic state handling when multiple language states coexist

None of these exist in the codebase. Specific evidence:

- **No `--by-language` flag**: The scan parser at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups.py` defines `--path`, `--no-badge`, `--badge-path`, `--skip-slow`, `--profile`, `--force-resolve`, and `--lang-opt` — no `--by-language`. The status parser only has `--state` and `--json`.

- **No multi-language scorecard generation**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_artifacts.py` calls a single `generate_scorecard(state, badge_path)` with one state and one path. There is no loop over detected languages, no `scorecard-{lang}.png` templating.

- **No per-language score reporting pipeline**: A grep for `by.language`, `by_language`, `per.language`, `multi.language` across the source tree returns only unrelated hits (comments about jscpd being "per-language", tree-sitter "per-language query definitions", etc.). No logic for running or rendering scores split by language.

- **What does exist (not the feature)**: `resolve_scorecard_lang()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard_parts/dimensions.py` detects the *current* scan's language to pick the correct subjective dimension ordering for a single scorecard. `auto_detect_lang()` in `languages/_framework/resolution.py` picks one dominant language from a project. These are single-language helpers, not the multi-language breakdown the issue requires.

---

**Verdict: Fully done** (with a very minor output gap vs. the spec).

Every core requirement from issue #157 is implemented:

**Requirement 1 — Plan-drained gate for `--prepare` / `--run-batches` / `--external-start`**

Implemented in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/preflight.py` via `review_rerun_preflight()`. All three entry paths call it in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/entrypoint.py` (lines 101, 112, 160). The gate calls `build_work_queue(..., include_subjective=False)` and exits with code 1 if `total > 0`, printing the count and scored dimension names.

**Requirement 2 — Auto-clear stale subjective entries before rerun**

`clear_stale_subjective_entries()` (preflight.py lines 19-43) strips `needs_review_refresh`, `stale_since`, and `refresh_reason` from assessments, with optional per-dimension targeting. It is called at the end of `review_rerun_preflight()` whether the gate passed or was force-bypassed, and the cleaned state is saved.

**Requirement 3 — `--force-review-rerun` escape hatch**

Registered in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py` (line 164) as `--force-review-rerun` (action=store_true). The gate checks `args.force_review_rerun` and skips the queue check when True, printing a yellow warning.

**Tests — All acceptance criteria covered**

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_review_preflight.py` has 30+ tests including:
- `test_blocked_when_open_objective_items` — blocked rerun with open plan
- `test_allowed_when_queue_empty` — allowed rerun with empty plan
- `test_clears_stale_on_gate_pass` / `test_force_review_rerun_still_clears_stale` — stale cleanup
- `test_force_review_rerun_bypasses_check` — override path
- Mode-dispatch tests confirming preflight is called for prepare/run-batches/external-start and skipped for import/validate-import/external-submit/merge

**Minor gap**: The issue spec asked the blocked message to also print "top remaining items" and "exact commands to finish or resolve them." The current blocked output only prints the count and scored dimension names — it doesn't enumerate the top N open items or suggest specific `desloppify fix` / `desloppify resolve` commands. This is a cosmetic/UX enhancement, not a structural gap.

def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    matched_ids = [
        finding_id
        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
    ]
    now = utc_now()
    for finding_id in matched_ids:
        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
        finding["suppressed"] = True
        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
        if finding.get("status") in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
            finding["status"] = "open"
            finding["resolved_at"] = None
            finding["note"] = (
                "Suppressed by ignore pattern — remains unresolved for score integrity"
            )
    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats

    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
    validate_state_invariants(state)
    return len(matched_ids)


def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
    ensure_state_defaults(state)
```

1→"""Compact queue table renderer for ``plan queue``."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, print_table
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
    12→
    13→
    14→def _truncate(text: str, width: int) -> str:
    15→    if len(text) <= width:
    16→        return text
    17→    return text[: width - 1] + "\u2026"
    18→
    19→
    20→def _cluster_tier_label(item: dict) -> str:
    21→    """Compute a tier label from cluster members, e.g. 'T2' or 'T1-T3'."""
    22→    members = item.get("members", [])
    23→    if not members:
    24→        return ""
    25→    tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in members}
    26→    lo, hi = min(tiers), max(tiers)
    27→    if lo == hi:
    28→        return f"T{lo}"
    29→    return f"T{lo}-T{hi}"
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_queue(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Render a compact table of all upcoming queue items."""
    34→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    35→    state = runtime.state
    36→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    top = getattr(args, "top", 30)
    40→    tier_filter = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    41→    cluster_filter = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
    42→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
    43→
    44→    plan = load_plan()
    45→    plan_data: dict | None = None
    46→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
    47→        plan_data = plan
    48→
    49→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster
    50→    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
    51→    if plan_data and not cluster_filter:

I now have enough information to give a definitive verdict. Here is the analysis:

---

## Verdict: Partially Done

The issue has five numbered proposals. Here is the status of each:

### Proposal 1 — Score-band finding contracts: PARTIALLY DONE

Two of the three bands are implemented:

- **score < 85 requires at least one finding** — implemented. `LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD = 85.0` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/feedback_contract.py`, enforced by `_validate_assessment_feedback()` in `import_helpers.py` (lines 532-557).
- **score < 100 requires explicit feedback** — implemented. `ASSESSMENT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD = 100.0`, checked in the same function.
- **overall strict score < 70 requires a cross-module/architectural finding** — NOT implemented. There is no check that validates the overall strict score against finding scope/type.

### Proposal 2 — Root-cause classification fields on findings: NOT DONE

The issue proposes adding normalized fields to the finding schema: `severity_tier` (t1..t4), `scope` (`local`, `module`, `cross_module`, `system`), `cause_level` (`symptom`, `root_cause`), `impact`. None of these fields exist in `ReviewFindingPayload` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/contracts.py`. The schema has `impact_scope` (values: `local`, `module`, `subsystem`, `codebase`) used in batch prompt templates and batch scoring, but it is a prompt-level/scoring hint, not a validated import contract field, and there is no `cause_level` or `severity_tier`.

### Proposal 3 — Validate score/finding consistency on import: PARTIALLY DONE

The `_validate_assessment_feedback()` function in `import_helpers.py` enforces:
- score < 85 without a finding for that dimension → hard fail
- score < 100 without feedback → hard fail

But it does NOT enforce:
- score < 70 without a `cause_level=root_cause` finding (because that field doesn't exist)
- only low-tier findings with very low score (no `severity_tier` field)

The error messages that do exist are actionable (e.g., `"assessments below 85.0 must include at least one finding..."`).

### Proposal 4 — Prompt/rubric updates for reviewers: DONE

`global_prompt_contract()` in `feedback_contract.py` (lines 35-60) instructs reviewers to think structurally, identify root causes, use `root_cause_cluster` to connect symptom findings, and scores below thresholds must have findings. The `batch_prompt_template.py` also includes root-cause language. This matches the spirit of the proposal.

### Proposal 5 — Configurable thresholds: NOT DONE

The issue requests `root_cause_threshold`, `minimum_findings_threshold`, and `validation_mode: warn | fail` exposed in config. All thresholds are module-level constants, not config-driven. There is no `warn` vs `fail` mode; the import always hard-fails on violations (with the `--manual-override` escape hatch).

---

### Summary table

| Requirement | Status |
|---|---|
| score < 85 requires at least one finding per dimension | Done |
| score < 70 requires root-cause finding per dimension | Not done (no `cause_level` field) |
| overall strict < 70 requires cross-module/architectural finding | Not done |
| Finding schema fields: `cause_level`, `severity_tier`, `scope`, `impact` | Not done |
| Import validation with actionable error messages | Partially done (basic band checks exist, root-cause checks absent) |
| Prompt/rubric instructs reviewers on root-cause thinking | Done |
| Configurable thresholds (`root_cause_threshold`, `minimum_findings_threshold`) | Not done |
| Validation mode `warn | fail` | Not done |

The core infrastructure for score-band enforcement is in place (proposals 3 and 4 are partially/fully done), but the deeper requirements — the `cause_level`/`severity_tier`/`scope` finding schema extensions that make deterministic root-cause validation possible, the < 70 overall strict score architectural finding requirement, and configurable thresholds — have not been implemented.

+def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
+    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
+    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
+        return True
+    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
+    return (
+        file_path.startswith(prefix)
+        or file_path == scan_path
+        or file_path == "."
+    )
+
+
+def open_scope_breakdown(
+    findings: dict[str, Finding],
+    scan_path: str | None,
+    *,
+    detector: str | None = None,
+) -> dict[str, int]:
+    """Return open-finding counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
+    in_scope = 0
+    out_of_scope = 0
+
+    for finding in findings.values():
+        if finding.get("status") != "open":
+            continue
+        if detector is not None and finding.get("detector") != detector:
+            continue
+        file_path = str(finding.get("file", ""))
+        if finding_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
+            in_scope += 1
+        else:
+            out_of_scope += 1
+
+    return {
+        "in_scope": in_scope,
+        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
+        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
     }

def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    matched_ids = [
        finding_id
        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
    ]
    now = utc_now()
    for finding_id in matched_ids:
        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
        finding["suppressed"] = True
        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
        if finding.get("status") in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
            finding["status"] = "open"
            finding["resolved_at"] = None
            finding["note"] = (
                "Suppressed by ignore pattern — remains unresolved for score integrity"
            )
    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats

    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
    validate_state_invariants(state)
    return len(matched_ids)


def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""

Here is the full analysis.

---

## Verdict: SUBSTANTIALLY DONE — two acceptance criteria not fully met

The living plan engine described in issue 153 is largely implemented. The core infrastructure, most subcommands, and the plan-aware surfaces are all present. Below is a criterion-by-criterion breakdown.

---

### What IS fully implemented

**1. Persistent plan state (`engine/_plan/` package)**

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py` defines `PlanModel` with `queue_order`, `skipped`, `overrides`, `clusters`, `superseded`, and `active_cluster`. Stored in `.desloppify/plan.json`. Schema is v2 with v1 migration.

**2. Reordering and plan editing commands**

`plan move` — supports `before`, `after`, `top`, `bottom`, `up N`, `down N` positions with full `operations.py` backing logic. `plan note` and `plan describe` set per-finding annotations. `plan skip` / `unskip` / `done` / `reopen` / `focus` are all implemented in `override_handlers.py`. The issue proposed `plan edit --move`; the implementation uses `plan move` as the verb instead, which is functionally equivalent.

**3. Plan/queue sync semantics (`plan sync`)**

This is **not a standalone CLI command** named `plan sync`. However, the behavior it describes is implemented automatically: `reconcile_plan_after_scan()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile.py` runs after every scan (called from `scan_workflow.py`). It preserves manual ordering/context, moves disappeared findings to `superseded` with candidate remapping hints, and resurfaces stale temporary skips. The comments from the issue owner explicitly requested the superseded/needs-remap behavior — this is done.

The **gap**: there is no explicit `desloppify plan sync` command the user can invoke manually between scans to get a sync report. Reconciliation is only triggered by running `scan`.

**4. `next` integration with active plan**

`next.py` loads the living plan unconditionally and passes `plan_data` to `build_work_queue()`. `_apply_plan_order()` in `_work_queue/core.py` reorders items by `queue_order` before returning them. The active cluster auto-scopes `next` output. Plan metadata (description, note, cluster) is annotated onto each item.

The **gap**: the issue specified `next --from-plan` (default when plan exists) and `next --from-queue` (fallback) as explicit flags, and a "transparent hint when queue ranking and manual plan ranking diverge." Neither the flags nor the divergence hint exists. The plan is always applied silently when present — there is no `--from-plan` / `--from-queue` flag and no divergence warning.

**5. Clustering**

Full clustering is implemented: `plan cluster create/add/remove/delete/move/show/list`, auto-clustering via `auto_cluster_findings()` (runs post-scan by detector/subtype grouping), cluster-scoped `next` via `--cluster`, and focused cluster via `plan focus`. This substantially covers both the proposal and the comments requesting cluster-first strategy preservation.

**6. Context visibility in plan output**

`plan queue` and `next` render terminal output with `plan_description`, `plan_note`, `plan_cluster`, and skip metadata. `plan show` reports annotation counts.

**7. End-to-end fix loop**

`scan → plan → next → plan done → scan` loop works. `plan done` marks findings as fixed with attestation and shows score movement and next step. `resolve` also purges from the plan. The loop uses only `plan` + `next` + `plan done`/`fix` — no external docs required.

---

### Acceptance criteria NOT yet met

**1. `next --from-plan` flag and divergence hint (Acceptance criterion: "`next --from-plan` follows manual plan order, not just algorithmic queue rank")**

The behavior exists (plan order is always applied), but the explicit `--from-plan` / `--from-queue` flags are absent, as is the "transparent hint when queue ranking and manual plan ranking diverge." A user cannot opt out of plan ordering via a flag, and there is no feedback when the plan's ordering differs from what algorithmic ranking would have chosen.

**2. `plan suggest-meta` command (Acceptance criterion: "Plan supports both finding-level and meta-level items")**

Auto-clustering groups findings by detector/subtype automatically post-scan, but the issue also proposed:
- `desloppify plan suggest-meta` — a command proposing higher-level tasks by clustering repeated/related findings across modules.
- `desloppify plan add --type meta_task --title "..."` — a command to add first-class meta-level plan items (architecture epics, root-cause tracks) that are not tied to any specific finding ID.

Neither exists. The `ItemOverride` and `Cluster` schemas only track finding-sourced items. There is no `kind` field on plan items (`meta_task`, `refactor_epic`, `review_followup`) and no way to create a standalone plan item without a backing finding ID.

---

### Summary table

| Acceptance criterion | Status |
|---|---|
| User can reorder any plan item and order persists across scans | Done |
| User can add/update context notes on any plan item | Done (via `plan note` / `plan describe`) |
| `next --from-plan` follows manual plan order | Partially done — behavior works, flag missing, no divergence hint |
| `plan sync` preserves context and reports mapping/conflict events | Partially done — runs automatically on every scan, no standalone `plan sync` command |
| Plan supports finding-level items | Done |
| Plan supports meta-level items (`meta_task`, `refactor_epic`, etc.) | Not done — no `plan add`, no `plan suggest-meta`, no non-finding plan items |
| End-to-end fix loop using only `plan`, `next`, and `resolve` | Done (`plan done` replaces `resolve` in the loop) |

Now I have a comprehensive picture. Let me compile the final verdict.

Here is the complete assessment:

---

## Verdict: Partially Done (core bug fixed; some issue items remain outstanding)

### What the issue described

Issue 168 identified two root causes of score corruption after `ignore` pattern changes:

1. `remove_ignored_findings()` was reopening resolved findings (`fixed`/`auto_resolved`/`false_positive`) back to `open` status before marking them suppressed — causing score collapse.
2. No `needs_rescan` guardrail existed to flag scores as stale after pattern changes.

The issue proposed 5 concrete fixes and 1 nice-to-have.

---

### What has been fixed (the core bug)

**Root cause 1 — the reopen-on-suppress behavior — is FIXED.**

In commit `6268a61`, the block that forced status back to `open` was deleted from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`:

```python
# REMOVED in 6268a61:
if finding.get("status") in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
    finding["status"] = "open"
    finding["resolved_at"] = None
    finding["note"] = (
        "Suppressed by ignore pattern — remains unresolved for score integrity"
    )
```

Now `remove_ignored_findings()` only sets `suppressed=True` and leaves the status intact. The same commit also fixed `open_scope_breakdown()` to skip suppressed findings.

**`needs_rescan` guardrail — is implemented.**

`cmd_ignore_pattern` (line 176 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`) and `cmd_exclude` (line 18 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/exclude_cmd.py`) both set `config["needs_rescan"] = True` after pattern changes. `check_config_staleness()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/tooling.py` returns the warning `"Config changed — scores may be stale. Run: desloppify scan"` and this warning surfaces in `status`, `next`, `show`, `plan`, and `ignore` command outputs. A successful scan clears the flag at line 419–424 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`.

**Regression tests — are present.**

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/state/test_suppression_scoring.py` covers the key scenarios:
- `TestRemoveIgnoredPreservesStatus` — verifies `fixed`/`auto_resolved`/`false_positive` findings stay in their resolved status after suppression (directly tests the issue's minimal repro).
- `TestIgnoreDoesNotCorruptScore` — end-to-end test that suppressed findings are invisible to all scoring paths.
- `TestUpsertPreservesResolvedStatus` — verifies the merge path also preserves resolved status when a finding is suppressed by an ignore pattern.

---

### What is NOT done (remaining gaps)

**Proposed fix 3 (transactional rebuild) — not implemented.** The issue asked for scores to be rebuilt "from fresh detector output in that post-change scan (transactional), instead of in-place status rewrites." The current implementation still mutates state in-place and defers the clean rebuild to the next scan. `needs_rescan` acts as a soft advisory flag, not a hard block.

**Proposed fix 2 (blocking authoritative scores until rescan) — not implemented.** `needs_rescan` surfaces a warning but does not block `status`, `strict_score`, or `objective_score` from being reported as authoritative. The issue asked to block score output as authoritative until a rescan completes.

**Proposed fix 4 (UX messaging) — partially done.** The success path still prints `"Removed {removed} matching findings from state."` (line 193 of `cmd.py`) even though findings are now properly suppressed (not removed) and resolved findings are no longer reopened. The issue asked this to be replaced with precise language ("Suppressed N findings" + note about no reopening).

**Nice-to-have: `desloppify rebaseline` command — not implemented.** No such command exists anywhere in the codebase.

---

### Summary table

| Issue requirement | Status |
|---|---|
| Stop reopening resolved findings on suppress | Done (commit 6268a61) |
| Set `needs_rescan` flag on pattern changes | Done |
| Surface `needs_rescan` warning in commands | Done |
| Clear flag after successful scan | Done |
| Transactional rebuild from fresh scan (not in-place mutation) | Not done |
| Hard-block authoritative scores until rescan | Not done |
| Fix UX message ("Removed..." -> "Suppressed...") | Partially — warning added, message text unchanged |
| Regression tests for broad ignore after resolved findings | Done |
| `desloppify rebaseline` command | Not done |
